- Structured output on servers built from source: `zig build grammar` installs libtfgrammar beside the native server on every OS (on Linux it went to `lib/`, where the server does not look), and a server without it says so once at startup, naming the fix. Docs: what `--parallel` caps, the startup line that reports how many streams the engine took, and `/health`'s live counts.

- Docs: what a run past two ranks reports (#1201, thanks to @flashosophy), the 27B recipe's native CUDA backend and the formats it reads (#1146, thanks to @jschmied), a GPU clock cap for GB10s in the runbook (#1110, thanks to @Bizuayeu), and the tensor-parallel fabric's RoCE attribution (#1128, thanks to @jayleaton).

## 1.1.2

- Chat prompts keep request text out of the added vocabulary: a control-token spelling in a message, tool definition, tool result, reasoning field or decision label is encoded as ordinary text, while chat templates still see the real text. A request whose text would still encode to an added-token id is refused instead, explicit `--vision` refuses to start when an image marker is not an added token, and LFM2's automatic image input turns off with a warning rather than failing the load (#1117). Thanks to @Acermax.

- Tool parameters whose schema is a union (`oneOf`, `anyOf`, `allOf` or a type list) or a local `$ref` are decoded as JSON when the schema takes a non-string value, streamed and not; text that is not JSON keeps what the model wrote (#1121). Thanks to @aartiles.

- Share Flash Next's existing proposal table and exact stored rows without changing its default IDs or packs.

- Prepare exact stored proposal head rows and shared scoring views without changing family defaults or precision.

- Prepare optional shared proposal routing for native and external draft heads, with full or absorb-only fallback.

- The optional 27B Neural Engine share uses a measured 32 MiB private-overhead price and reserves its full size in the prompt-cache budget, including cold builds (#643). Thanks to @RS91Dev.

- Shared draft rounds offer one deeper row every eighth round when the row cap permits, retaining normal pricing and trimming (#1059). Thanks to @plotarmordev.

- Nemotron tensor parallelism shares Flash Next's NCCL 2.30.0 minimum, refusing older loaded libraries before rank setup (#1060). Thanks to @plotarmordev.

- Linux checkpoint hashing reads runtime SHA2 capabilities through libc when libc owns process startup.

- Streaming research waits for the HTTP response head before sending chat, Messages or Responses keepalives.

- Plain Qwen3.8-27B Metal prompt caching can copy up to four interior DeltaNet marks without separate prompt calls, within the memory bound (#548, #851). Thanks to @jschmied.

- Streaming research replies keep late context-length refusals on the stream while retaining pre-token keepalives (#912). Thanks to @cwschroeder and @akol1.

- Shared CUDA sliding-window attention uses bounded split launches, with sigmoid-routing and dense-row contract fixtures (#798). Thanks to @jschmied.

- Shared CUDA tree and DFlash block attention have exact-replay launch contracts and bounded resource records (#746). Thanks to @jschmied.

- An opt-in GLM CUDA FP8 latent-cache implementation is prepared behind --kv-cache fp8. BF16 remains the default; native quality and device qualification are pending.

- GLM CUDA prompt diagnostics under TF_GLM_PROF report per-rank chunk stages, pieced transport phases and event waits. Timing is diagnostic; context ownership is unchanged.

- Shared serving and prompt scheduling have an embedding request contract with float/base64 output and declared MRL dimensions (#1035). Native EmbeddingGemma-2 loading and device qualification remain pending. Thanks to @jschmied for the RFC.

- On the 27B CUDA path, TF_TIMING reports offered and kept rows, capture bucket and forward time for each verification frame.

- Checkpoint hashing uses a bounded file pool and runtime SHA acceleration, preserving digest identities and atomic cache publication.

- Qwen3.8-27B on CUDA sizes recurrent verification scratch by its live windows and keeps it between rounds, freeing it when the engine goes idle or a new size would pass what its live caches budget, retaining fp32 state and exact rollback.

- Qwen3.8-27B on CUDA can prefer verified context copies over neural proposals through the shared lane core. Copy rounds keep the drafter's committed target rows current and resume neural drafting after rejection.

- Flash Next can reuse precise rotary values across target layers and MTP calls on tensor-unit Metal devices. Admission keeps the direct path when the optional table does not fit.

- Cluster checkpoint headers use the core safetensors parser and validate tensor ranges against the actual file length.

- HTTP serving closes unfinished replies, reports token staging allocation failures, releases SSE frame storage after each write, and shares one public address policy between media and web fetches.

- Mac source build instructions name full Xcode and its Metal Toolchain component, and the build example uses the package manifest's version (#1049). Thanks to @akol1.

- HTTP serving bounds total request-head and body reads, caps open connections at 128, and returns 503 with Retry-After when full. Inference and streamed replies keep their existing timing policy.

- Living Weights implementation and safety work is already in the 1.1.1 tree. Its model, retention and save/restart qualification remains pending; the #1029 lesson-counter repair is excluded and remains held.

- Flash Next on Metal checks its weights, startup scratch and requested storage against the RAM/GPU-bounded load ceiling before loading (#1039). Thanks to @hudsonwa.

- Qwen on CUDA keeps DFlash and MTP proposal and live context projections on native arithmetic regardless of shared frame size, so a stream's drafts do not depend on which streams share its rounds. On GB10 a shared frame's rows go through one launch of 32-, 64- or 128-row verify tiles, each row with the one-row decode's bits, and the target's wider verify frames take the same tiles. Prompt ingestion retains its historical arithmetic.

- Qwen on single-GPU HIP polls cancellation inside prompt chunks, releasing cancelled work at layer boundaries (#1028). Thanks to @ThinkOffApp.

- Metal serving keeps weights, stream state and draft, image and speech working buffers resident between requests within the memory bound. GLM enables the shared set by default; `GLM_RESIDENCY=0` turns it off.

- Flash Next on Metal fills response prompts through the shared lane host, with at most 32 prompt rows per choice beside a decoder. A long prompt started alone can hand unfinished work to the host when another request arrives, retaining its session and MTP lookahead (#1032). Thanks to @tpischke.

- Qwen3.8-27B on M1 to M4 takes prompt chunks through the M5's prompt attention, on simdgroup matrices: on an M3 Ultra a cold 32,768-token prompt runs at 269 tokens/s instead of 176, and 8,192 tokens at 295 instead of 251. Each prompt row used to read every key alone through the decode tail kernel. A row's bits still never depend on how its prompt is cut, its error against float64 stays at bf16's half step, and decoded rows are unchanged (#918). Thanks to @astro-15-alive.

- Flash Next on CUDA takes prompt rows through factored tiles: dense matmuls, routed experts, hyper-connection read-out, router logits, the recurrence at two threads a state row, indexer scores and 1,024-row attention launches, each bit-equal to the 1.1.0 kernel it replaces and registered with the kernel contract. The tiles run only where native glue owns every operation they replace, and `TF_FLASHNEXT_CHUNK` takes 1 to 16,384 rows, refusing anything else before loading; CUDA startup reports the chunk actually loaded (#1046). Thanks to @Mirrdhyn.

- Flash Next on CUDA serves structured output and required tool calls: the grammar masks the first draw and every draw in a verification window (#1062). Thanks to @Mirrdhyn.

- `--thinking-reserve N` keeps `max_tokens` for the answer when thinking is on: lane engines allow up to N more tokens for reasoning, within the context window, and end the reply when the answer reaches `max_tokens`. It is off by default, and other engines say at startup that it is not applied (#1064). Thanks to @BobClawblaw.

- `--loop-guard` also closes reasoning that repeats a long passage verbatim (a whole period and at least 1,024 tokens), not only short cycles. It still acts only while the think block is open, and reserves its index before tokens are published (#1075). Thanks to @BobClawblaw.

- With `--vision`, startup warns when the chat template renders an image part without an image placeholder, naming `--chat-template`, and an image request whose template writes none says so rather than reading as an engine failure (#1076). Thanks to @BobClawblaw.

- For stored bf16 `eh_proj` weights, GLM-5.3-Flash on Metal runs the MTP draft head's prompt-side `eh_proj` on a tensor-unit tile kernel by default; if its pipeline cannot build, it warns once and uses the scalar path, and `GLM_EHPROJ_NAX=0` selects the scalar path. Quantized `eh_proj` weights keep their quantized path (#1066). Thanks to @EugeneClaw.

- GLM tensor parallelism on CUDA lets waiting ranks sleep between requests instead of polling at full load: rank 0 sends an idle step when its last stream ends and wakes them before its next step, and a rank that fails while the others sleep still stops them (#1053). Thanks to @Bizuayeu.

- On M1, quantized matmul tiles covering 16 rows use at most four simdgroups when the grid has at least 512 groups and the K-split count is at most 16; partial sums keep their fixed reduction order, so results are unchanged (#1055). Thanks to @mrv777.

- Nemotron on CUDA runs the RADIO vision tower once per image prompt instead of twice (#1057), and shared windows of several sequences on one GPU run the latent shared expert on its side stream again, with tensor-parallel runs keeping the main stream (#1058). Thanks to @plotarmordev.

- Qwen3.5-family MoE models on CUDA route only a captured bucket's live rows through the experts: padding rows no longer reach the router, top-k, plan or combine, so their experts' weights are not read (#1047). Thanks to @jschmied.

- Shared CUDA operators gain MLX's bf16 rounding steps, ready for families to adopt: residual tails with an optional branch norm and layer scalar (#1056), GeGLU and rounded SwiGLU rows (#1068), affine embedding rows at 2 to 6 and 8 bits with a bf16 scale (#1070), and q/k rows to head dimension 512 with a unit V norm (#1071). Each is registered with the kernel contract, and existing kernels are unchanged. Thanks to @jschmied.

- The `ck_lane` kernel-contract fixture sizes its zero buffer for the input rows as well as the weights, fixing a panic in Debug and ReleaseSafe contract builds at 129-row shapes (#1073). Thanks to @jschmied.

- ROCm benchmark receipts name the GPU, read from rocminfo under HIP's device-visibility rules, and the CPU. An attached server can attest the checkpoint revision, GPU identity and receipt backend through a valid benchmark manifest; without one, the attached run stays unranked. Managed runs, inspect and publish say which facts the board ranks by a receipt lacks, before any upload. ROCm board publication remains disabled (#1043). Thanks to @ThinkOffApp.

- HIP refuses `--drafter` before loading, since it does not run a DFlash2 drafter yet; the refusal leaves the engine's native drafting settings unchanged. A managed ROCm run of `tensorfold benchmark` refuses a named external `--drafter` and records none; an attached run does not configure the server's drafter (#1044). Thanks to @ThinkOffApp.

- `tensorfold benchmark` keeps each attempt's draft counts (verify rounds, drafted and accepted tokens) in a local file beside the receipt, and reports no totals rather than overflowing when a sum would pass 64 bits; the published receipt is unchanged (#1045). Thanks to @ThinkOffApp.

- The Flash Next CUDA recipe adds single-GB10 measurements of the ModelOpt FP4-Local checkpoint, labelled with the earlier build they ran on (#1042). Thanks to @balancedscorpion and @akol1.

- Windows host builds and tests build again: dynamic libraries, clocks, environment reads and socket options go through the portable system layer, Flash Next's rank wire refuses on Windows, and POSIX-only tests skip there, while Linux and macOS run what they did (#1034). Thanks to @jayleaton.

- The session prefix store reserves its free lists before publishing a slot, so removal and rollback no longer allocate, with the two allocation-failure tests from #637. Thanks to @jayleaton.

- HTTP serving consumes and validates GET and DELETE bodies before routing, so a body cannot become a later request, refuses incomplete or malformed request heads and folded header lines, and bounds all chunk framing together. An API-key file that a group or other users can read or write is refused.

- `TENSORFOLD_REQUEST_LOG` writes only to a regular file that no one but its owner can read or write; it refuses a symbolic link or a shared file with one warning instead of appending prompts there, and creates a new log 0600.

- Research web redirects that leave the first URL's scheme, host or port drop the request's extra headers, such as a configured search key, for the rest of the chain.

- Model pulls refuse unsafe portable paths and fallback blob names, follow nested snapshot links at their real depth, and never write past a file's declared size. Model inspection refuses non-positive quantization bits or group sizes.

- Checkpoint shard indexes need checked JSON types and names inside the model folder, and checkpoint writes stage in an exclusive sibling file opened without following links before replacing the target atomically. The secondary Metal, GLM, Flash Next and Kimi readers share the bounded safetensors header checks and refuse tensors whose payloads overlap.

- HTTP serving and the Kolibri runner share one end-of-sequence resolver that merges the model's and the generation config's EOS ids, so a declared second end token also stops HTTP replies. A present EOS entry with a negative, fractional, boolean or out-of-range id refuses loading.

- Kolibri keeps its 16 verification rows past the advertised window in its caches, memory pricing and admission, so a request at the advertised edge no longer fails admission. The advertised context is unchanged.

- GLM on CUDA keeps each committed prompt prefix in bounded, preallocated snapshot pages instead of its whole context arena, so saved prompt states stay inside the prompt-cache budget; prompt arithmetic is unchanged (#993). Thanks to @ss-cong.

- GLM on CUDA runs the uneven KDA input projections of TP=3 (22, 21 and 21 heads) on the existing row-major matmuls instead of refusing them; aligned shares keep the tiled path. TP=3 device qualification is pending (#1067). Thanks to @Bizuayeu.

- GLM-5.3 on Metal skips the sparse indexer's scoring pass while its 2,048-key top-k already holds every causal key, where those scores were never read. Longer windows are unchanged, and the timing gain is not yet measured (#1054). Thanks to @Chris9121.

- Linux video looks for `libtfvideo.so` in the executable's `../lib` directory, then beside the executable, then on the loader's path, as capability discovery does. Real video through that layout remains unchecked (#1107). Thanks to @Bizuayeu.

- Qwen3.8-27B on Metal takes prompt matmuls of 16 K chunks in 16 by 32 tiles, each simdgroup adding a pair of chunks, after a once-per-shape check on the chip that the pairs give the earlier tiles' bits; shapes that fail it keep the earlier tiles.

- Flash Next on Metal releases its buffers, pipelines, mappings and files after a partial load or at shutdown, draining its queues first.

- Shared lane rounds let other streams use spare rows when a forced window does not fit, keeping their waiting age, and shared prompt scheduling prefers admitted prompts with less work left, keeping foreground preference and bounded waits.

- Shared lanes reserve context, paired-row and loop-close storage before grammar transitions and token publication, so an allocation failure publishes no rejected token and keeps a pending thinking close.

- Prompt-cache recall picks a disk restore by its estimated time saved over the best RAM state, within the memory budget, and keeps the longest compatible prefix when timing rates are unknown. The tokenizer's memo cache keeps recently used segments when it evicts.

- `tf-qwen27-run --profile-prompt` splits a timed Qwen3.8-27B prompt's GPU time by class. It is off by default, and profiling slows the prompt. The cache and concurrent benchmark tools time a whole workload from its first request to its last completion, keep failed workers in the result and refuse incomplete throughput totals.

- Prepare immutable shared proposal-vocabulary artifacts and an offline `tools/draft_vocab.py responses` builder from committed response IDs. No artifact is activated and defaults are unchanged.

- The README opens with a pinned native-model quick start for Mac and Linux and a table of native model choices.

- The app checks credentials, remote access, MCP launches and workspace files before use: restored masked credentials stay bound to their endpoint and account, revoking a paired device or rotating the owner credential ends its live streams, a stopped MCP server starts only after native confirmation of its whole launch configuration, Git hygiene and artifact reads stay inside the workspace, and verified downloads refuse invalid sizes, looping pagination and links that leave the configured Hub endpoint.

- The app puts its planning-only note in the first system message, does not retry a recognized chat-template refusal, and retries a refused reasoning effort once at the nearest level the server names, saying so in the timeline (#884). Thanks to @Gogo6969.

## 1.1.1

- The app checks models through HTTPS Hub fake-IP proxies in both address families and honours one validated `HF_ENDPOINT` for checks and downloads. Network-policy refusals name the DNS address and proxy remedy (#1037). Thanks to @samwang0041-star.

- Flash Next on Metal uses precise exp2, sine and cosine for rotary preparation and index pooling, including M-RoPE (#986, #1005). Thanks to @BobClawblaw, @cwschroeder and @sethforprivacy, retaining the original YaRN attribution.
- GLM-5.3-Flash NVFP4 loads and serves the whole checkpoint on two GB10s at TP=2 in a contributed regression run on shipped 1.1.0: 302.1 seconds to load, one token hash per prompt across three runs, and a passphrase found in 199,307 tokens. Scored prompts and more than two ranks were not covered (#1040). Thanks to @Bizuayeu.

- Qwen3.8-27B on Metal keeps its Neural Engine prompt share off by default on M5. Its fp16 prompt rows can change replies, so the default follows the no-quality-tradeoffs rule. `TF_ANE_COLUMNS=N` opts in; M1 to M4 already use the GPU alone. The load receipt reports requested and loaded columns, fp16 program-weight bytes and exact-output qualification.

- Source builds report the release version. A source build of 1.1.0 reported 1.0.12 because its package manifest still carried the older version.
- Qwen on CUDA uses larger verification trees, shared rounds across streams and batched DFlash proposals. Captured launches follow the live row and window sizes. A lone stream chooses its draft width from measured complete-round costs, with separate retry cooldowns for widths that did not repay their cost; straight chains avoid the branch planner and contiguous drafter rows avoid a redundant gather (#978). Thanks to @BobClawblaw and the shared CUDA contributors.
- DeepSeek-V4.1 CUDA pieces land in 1.1.1, but no release binary serves DeepSeek checkpoints until whole-model gate #827 passes. Its implementation includes shared checkpoint planning and Engram token maps, paged and split KV, prompt-cache sessions, keyed sampling and drafting through the lane scheduler. Devices older than sm_90 refuse before family image loading. The shared configuration and host references accompany the CUDA family; a complete DeepSeek Metal backend remains separate (#667, #668, #671, #674, #686, #688, #691, #693, #694, #695, #699, #702, #703, #707, #727, #824, #825, #826, #827). Thanks to @jayleaton and @akol1, retaining Turboderp's ExLlamaV3 attribution and the common EXL3 contributors' credits.
- Kolibri-1's text research checkpoint serves on native CUDA through the lane scheduler, with prompt caching, shared rounds and verified copy drafts. EAGLE-3 and a neural drafter remain unsupported (#991, #481). Thanks to @jschmied and the shared operator contributors.
- GLM CUDA stepped serving interleaves fresh prompt chunks with running decode. Whole-prompt mode can batch eligible fresh text prompts across ranks, keeping each sequence's final prompt row and first draft separate; each pass holds at most 16 prompts and 2,048 rows. Images and resumed prompts keep their own paths (#1014). GLM CUDA prompt projections use one K slice at every operator row count by default. This equal-precision reduction regrouping can change replies versus 1.1.0, including long or resumed prompts whose internal projection calls have fewer than 128 rows. `TF_GLM_PROMPT_K=shape`, with `TF_GLM_MM=1` on every rank, instead uses shape-selected K slices throughout prompt projections, including full blocks; it does not restore 1.1.0's mixed row-count policy. Verify and draft windows retain their existing policy. Thanks to @BobClawblaw.
- GLM on CUDA has an experimental prompt projection policy, `TF_GLM_PROMPT_K=shape`, which chooses K slices from the weight's shape. It requires `TF_GLM_MM=1` and the same setting on every rank. It is off by default, and its different reduction grouping can change near-ties; verify and draft windows keep their existing policy. Thanks to @BobClawblaw and the TensorFold maintainers.
- GLM on CUDA returns prompt log probabilities for nonstreaming `/v1/completions` requests with `prompt_logprobs`. Scoring storage is reserved before collective work, and ordinary requests keep their existing path (#1016). Thanks to @Bizuayeu.
- GLM on CUDA accepts schema-constrained structured output and the guided-output options, including a mask on the first generated token and independent grammar state for each sequence. Drafting observes the same constraints (#1017). Thanks to @Bizuayeu.
- Required and named tool calls on GLM CUDA use the model's tool markup, with schema-constrained argument values and matching parsing on the server (#1018). Thanks to @Bizuayeu.
- The common CUDA runtime has a keyed graph cache and a device arena, enabled only by families that install them. Failed captures or allocations release unpublished resources, and rank agreement keeps tensor-parallel cache decisions together (#635, #682). Thanks to @jayleaton and the shared runtime contributors.
- The common tensor-parallel fabric supports process bootstrap, NCCL, mailbox and RoCE collectives, checks that ranks agree on their settings, and propagates a failed peer instead of leaving the other rank waiting (#636). Thanks to @jayleaton, retaining the shared transport and RoCE attributions.
- Paged-KV families can use shared session memory with prefix reuse, a bounded RAM residency budget and a streamed disk tier on Linux. Failed saves and restores retain the owners needed for a retry (#637). Thanks to @jayleaton and @cwschroeder.
- On SIGTERM or SIGINT, the server gives requests in progress up to `TF_DRAIN_S` to finish, 20 seconds by default, while new engine requests get 503 with `Retry-After`. A second stop signal cuts the drain short; kept states drain before the engine closes (#663). Thanks to @jayleaton.
- Silent streaming responses send keepalives before their first token, every 15 seconds by default. `TF_SSE_KEEPALIVE_S` changes the interval; zero disables it (#663). Thanks to @jayleaton.
- A failed CUDA kernel launch names the kernel, grid, block and shared-memory size alongside the driver error, so a load or inference failure identifies the launch that failed (#635). Thanks to @jayleaton.
- Native CUDA refuses an unsupported lane policy instead of accepting an option that the selected family ignores. Kolibri receives the common lane options. Thanks to @ajensenwaud and @jayleaton.
- CUDA launch lookup can use a runtime-parameter variant for an argument of 1 when no suitable folded-one variant exists. Both preferred matching passes run first, each fallback is logged, and a launch with no matching variant still fails with its argument dump (#1020, #1003). Thanks to @GustavBlack and @cwschroeder.
- New benchmark runs use `community-v2`: a sampled reply that stops naturally after at least 128 tokens counts as complete, and its rate uses the tokens actually delivered. Greedy replies still need the full requested count, and empty replies still fail. Existing v1 receipts retain their rules; v2 runs can be saved and inspected locally, with publication disabled until the receiver and separate rankings support them (#969). Thanks to @BlivionIaG and @easthambuilds.
- The benchmark sends its code workload through `/v1/chat/completions` when the native family declares that it accepts only chat. An attached server uses that route only with `--chat-only`; an empty reply never triggers a route change. V2 receipts record the route, with the prompt and sampling settings unchanged (#1019). Thanks to @BobClawblaw, @BlivionIaG and @easthambuilds, and @jayleaton for the DeepSeek family declaration.
- Qwen3.8-27B on Metal allocates DeltaNet rollback scratch only for the slots whose verification windows need it, instead of reserving a full second state bank at load. Idle or lone use can release that scratch after the previous round has been kept. Recurrence arithmetic and saved prompt states are unchanged. Thanks to the TensorFold maintainers and the Qwen contributors.
- Request completion lines report `ttft`, `decode`, `elapsed` and `queued` seconds from that request's own timestamps, alongside its token rate. Queue time ends at first lane admission, and a cached prompt keeps its own timing (#1025). Thanks to @tpischke.
- Sampled native requests draw a fresh seed when neither the request nor the server supplies one, and completion lines record it for replay. An explicit request seed still overrides the server default. `--prompt-seed` restores the historical prompt-derived fallback; greedy sampling is unchanged (#1026). Thanks to @tpischke.
- The core has a host encoder and decoder for losslessly compressed `csf_32x2_v1` NVFP4 scales, with checked geometry, exception counts and allocation cleanup. This supplies the scale codec for future checkpoint consumers; it does not add a complete FP4-Local family loader (#1021). Thanks to @Bizuayeu, retaining local-inference-lab's format and reference attribution.
- CUDA's checkpoint weight estimate counts each file named in the shard index once, including files in subfolders. A malformed index or a missing or unreadable named shard refuses admission instead of producing an incomplete estimate; checkpoints without an index or weight map keep the flat-directory fallback (#1022). Thanks to @Bizuayeu.
- The core reads per-module ModelOpt `MIXED_PRECISION` recipes and reports unsupported declarations by module name. The existing NVFP4 reader keeps its behavior. This adds recipe descriptions for mixed-format consumers, with complete family loading still separate (#1023). Thanks to @Bizuayeu.
- The CUDA block-FP8 scale packer accepts each rank's global row and input origins, including tensor-parallel shares that begin inside a 128-element scale block. ModelOpt's four-dimensional block scales can be viewed as the equivalent two-dimensional layout without copying. Existing zero-origin packing is unchanged (#1024). Thanks to @Bizuayeu and @jschmied for the original fp8g layout in #548.

- Missing optional CUDA libraries name the library and the feature that needs it, with the dependencies also listed in `capabilities --json` and the runtime documentation. This makes failures such as GLM's vision path needing `libcublasLt.so.13` distinguishable from a text-only load failure (#1030). Thanks to @ss-cong for the report.
- The native CUDA host forwards the family's first-token handoff flag, so a family that enables it can deliver the first token before doing the next draft (#1031). Thanks to @jrb-ai.
- Contributor links in the README, CHANGELOG and 1.0.0 notes use @jrb-ai after the same account's handle change (#1036). Thanks to @jrb-ai.
- Qwen3.8-27B on CUDA caches all 64 measured decode-window costs between starts, keyed by the executable, bound device, checkpoint identities, KV format, taps and profiling capacity. A miss measures every width; a valid hit still warms this process's resources before ready, without repeating the timed sweep.
- Qwen3.8-27B on CUDA can open a short-context session with DFlash2: calibration uses a temporary cache when the configured context is too small for its warm-up windows. Calibration still finishes during open, before the lane thread starts.
- The runbook documents GB10 efficiency-core pinning with `taskset -c 0-4,10-14` or Docker's `--cpuset-cpus=0-4,10-14` to reduce CPU heat from the two spinning threads per CUDA rank. The reported heat and speed measurements retain their 1.0.12 plus #1016-#1018 scope (#1033). Thanks to @Bizuayeu.

## 1.1.0

- Structured output and forced tool calls work with Nemotron 3.5 Super VL (`nemotron_h_omni`), whose text vocabulary is in `llm_config`: the server read `vocab_size` only from `text_config` and the top level, and refused those requests (reported in #951). A `vocab_size` that is not a positive integer within the C ABI's range is skipped rather than cast (#953). Thanks to @swbiggart and @BobClawblaw.
- Accepted HTTP connections set `TCP_NODELAY`, so a streamed reply's small writes leave as they are written instead of waiting behind Nagle's algorithm; a connection that cannot be configured is closed and the server accepts the next one (#929). Thanks to @akol1.
- The tool-call parser finds namespaced closers (`</NAME:tool_call>`) in one pass over the reply, so a reply with many openers and no closers parses in linear time instead of searching the rest of the reply once per opener. Parsed calls are unchanged (#913). Thanks to @jishnuvenugopal.
- A chat template that builds a long string in a `namespace` (`{% set ns.out = ns.out + m.content %}`, or with `~`) appends to one growing buffer instead of copying the whole string at every step, so long histories render in linear memory. The rendered bytes are unchanged (#973). Thanks to @nmcbride.
- In the lane core, a pipelined stream adopted between rounds (detached and adopted again, or opened by another driver) starts in verify from its pending token; it read the missing mode as pipe and unwrapped a step it had never queued. No shipped family reaches that path yet (#928). Thanks to @chaog992.
- On Linux, a checkpoint read with O_DIRECT falls back to the page cache when the file system accepts the open but refuses the aligned read with EINVAL; the load used to fail (#916). Ours: the fallback is Linux's alone, so macOS builds it. Thanks to @plotarmordev.
- Nemotron on Metal loads on Macs without tensor units (M1 to M4) when its packed kernels cannot build there, as on macOS 26.3, whose tensor-op library has no bf16 x uint4 operation. Those chips never dispatch the packed kernels (their projections run on the row kernels), so a packed kernel that fails to build stays absent, with one warning; any other kernel, and every kernel on an M5, still fails the load (#955). Ours: a failed load releases the pipelines it built. Thanks to @jishnuvenugopal.
- The opt-in HIP build's Flash Next replays a round's graph only over the caches its pool slot was made with, and refuses caches made elsewhere (`CachesNotPooled`). `tf-flashnext-hip-test --repeat` takes slot 0 from the pool for every run, so a later run no longer replays the first run's graphs over freed buffers (#963). Thanks to @BlivionIaG.
- The CUDA runtime has `Context.shared`, the primary context of the calling thread's current device (device 0's when none is current, refused when the current context is not a primary one), and `Stream.borrowed`, a stream its caller owns, for a library that runs TensorFold's kernels inside a host framework's process (#946). Ours: fake-driver tests of the context and stream ownership branches, which do not stand for a host framework's own checks. Thanks to @drowzeys.
- `tf-cuda-test quant-ref` fails an output whose error against the float64 reference is not finite; a NaN after an out-of-bound element used to replace the largest error and pass. `zig build test-cuda-reference` runs the comparison's CPU tests, which `test-cuda-host` runs too (#933). Thanks to @cwschroeder.
- The CUDA kernel contract runs on sm_80 to sm_89: the qmm_group cluster kernel, which needs sm_90, is reported N/A there, and lane GEMV is held to the float64 bound instead of the cluster baseline's bits. sm_90 and newer are checked as before (#954). Thanks to @cwschroeder.
- `tools/zig/pack_compare.py` refuses a tensor whose byte range does not match its shape and dtype or runs past the file, where it compared the common prefix of a truncated pack; its tests run in a workflow scoped to its files (#919). Thanks to @cwschroeder.
- CONTRIBUTING.md and the README say that `zig build test` on macOS also runs the available Metal device checks, and name `zig build test-cuda-host` as the CUDA host subset (#957). Thanks to @cwschroeder.
- The app's model library reads each checkpoint metadata file (JSON, or a safetensors header) up to 64 MiB, where it stopped at 4 MiB and marked loadable checkpoints such as GLM-5.3-Flash (an 11.5 MiB shard index) incomplete; one scan reads at most 256 MiB of metadata in all, up from 32 (#967). Thanks to @heitke.
- The app's model library skips the AppleDouble companions (`._NAME`) that macOS writes beside each file on exFAT and FAT drives: it read them as weight shards and failed the checkpoint, and a stray one in a parent folder stopped the scan before the checkpoint below it (#970). Thanks to @heitke.
- The CUDA kernel contract keeps launch records per nvcc release: `registry.cuda` holds what nvcc 13.0, the release fatbins' compiler, compiles, and each later release lists only the launches it compiles differently (13.2, then 13.3 on top of it). `kernel-contract` and `kernel-contract-limits` read the release from each image's `.note.nv.tkinfo`, check against the newest set at or below it and print which set they used (#945). Ours: the sets per release, the records of the MXFP8 and FP32-output launches for both compilers, and a fatbin without the device's own SM image attributed only when its images agree on one compiler. Thanks to @akol1.
- The core decodes NVFP4 scale planes stored in a lossless compressed layout (byte-window bases with sorted exceptions) exactly, and a rank reads its tensor-parallel slice of an NVFP4 tensor exactly from plain or compressed scales, refusing ambiguous or half-present scale formats. No shipped checkpoint uses the layout yet (#904, #906). Thanks to @akol1.
- CUDA serves losslessly packed MXFP8 projections through the native qlinear, with their kernel-contract checks and launch records (#907), and the existing projection kernels can return FP32 outputs before the residual reduction; `zig build test-cuda-projection-fixture` poisons every dispatch's output and scratch before each launch, so a dispatch that writes nothing fails (#908). Thanks to @akol1 and @cwschroeder.
- The server admits a streamed request before it opens the response, so a refusal is still an HTTP 503, and health reports an engine that is closing or not ready, a drained Flash Next host among them, as unavailable (#912). Thanks to @akol1 and @cwschroeder.
- A test pins that a bounded-residency join which cannot grow its bookkeeping refuses whole, leaving nothing counted, staged or added (#909). Thanks to @BobClawblaw.
- In Living Weights, a new chat fact replaces an earlier one only when it changes that fact or the earlier fact no longer holds; answering one of the earlier fact's questions no longer retires it. When a told fact's questions do not name its subject, its reply gives the fact its key (#910). Ours: a judge's answer counts only as a whole yes or no from a reply that ended by itself, so "No idea" or a cut "No, it..." keeps the earlier fact. Thanks to @ZackSample.
- Nemotron on Metal: a new build keeps the chip's last window-cost table while probes of each kernel path still time within 15%, instead of timing the whole table again on its first start; a failed probe times it afresh. Costs steer the windows, never the tokens (#936). Ours: the shared rounds are probed too, and a timing cache that fails to make releases the ones already made. Thanks to @jishnuvenugopal and @cwschroeder.
- `tensorfold merge MODEL ADAPTER OUT` folds a PEFT LoRA into an MLX affine 4-bit checkpoint on the checkpoint's own scales, with no dense base, Python or GPU; `tensorfold merge DENSE ADAPTER OUT --quantize SHIPPED` instead quantizes the dense base plus the adapter fresh. On the author's Qwen3-4B checkpoint and adapter, one measured case, the fresh quantization kept a median 99.6% of the adapter's change against 0.1% for the fold, at 0.981 of the shipped quantization's pooled error. `tf-merge-check` gates both (#710). Ours: the fold as the default, a module found under either namespace's spelling, and two adapter modules for one projection refused. Thanks to @philip-pentatonic.
- GLM-5.3-Flash on CUDA: `TF_GLM_PREFILL_REDUCE=ring` passes split's exchanges along the ring, for a fabric where a rank reaches only its neighbours, and its sums keep split's rank order (#902). Ours: while the ranks' watch lines are open, an NCCL failure on one rank stops every rank instead of leaving them waiting; postings made through the shared group helper end their group when a post fails; and the families share the ring's plan. Thanks to @Bizuayeu.
- The lane host takes an opt-in round planner: a family can run prompts in pieces between the other streams' decode rounds, give each request its own copy proposer and trim each window's rows, and `halt` ends the rounds at the next boundary and fails what is left with its reason. A planner whose admitted callback fails ends each job it admitted once, as failed, and a failed round, cancellations and a close end every job even when no memory is left. No family uses the planner yet; the default loop serves as before (#861). Thanks to @jayleaton.
- CUDA's buffer counters keep the pinned host peak beside the device peak and can hand over an interval's peaks as they reset them; `tools/memory_receipts.py` runs one command, records its Linux resident peak and the allocator's load and cleanup receipts, and reports a total footprint as unsupported rather than adding the two (#865). Thanks to @jayleaton.
- A versioned drafter release format (`docs/drafter-release.md`): config.json names the target, its adapter and taps, `check` compares it with the loaded target and `checkRecording` with a `--record` recording, and `Release.open` reads a folder and checks every tensor's name and shape. Nothing serves a release yet (#893). Thanks to @jschmied.
- A recipe for training a block drafter from `--record` recordings into a drafter release, with what each step gained on Kolibri-1 (`docs/recipes/training-a-drafter.md`) (#894). Thanks to @jschmied.
- Qwen3.8-27B decode on Metal: K splits on M5 measured over every layer's matrices, an undrafted one-row round kept by its own verify with a DeltaNet step of three dispatches, and decode attention in 128-key chunks below position 8,192, chosen for each row by its own position so a window across 8,192 keeps every row's one-row bits. The chunks change the attention's summation order below 8,192 keys, on M5 too, so replies can differ from 1.0.12's; against float64 the new order's error is no larger, and drafted replies still equal `"draft": false` (#887). Thanks to @BobClawblaw.
- GLM-5.3-Flash on CUDA with `TF_GLM_PREFILL_REDUCE=split`, the default, exchanges a value with every other rank before the weights load and refuses a pair of ranks that cannot reach each other, instead of failing in the first prompt (#899). Ours: only direct split runs that probe, so `ring` starts on four ranks that reach only their neighbours, and the refusal names `ring` or `gather`; host tests over a fake fabric cover the refusal and the ring start, not a four-rank machine. Thanks to @Bizuayeu.
- Intel Arc Pro B70 (the XPU engine over Level Zero) checks and rounds up its sizes at open (`XPU_CHUNK` a multiple of 64 up to 8,192, a context up to 262,144), makes a stream's states whole or not at all, frees a snapshot only after its copies drain, reads safetensors headers within their files, and gives the card back at close; prompt chunks are cut on the 64-row grid at any length, and the prompt GDN's fp32 operands enter its fp16 DPAS as high and low terms after a power-of-two row scale. The author measured these on his B70 (#802). Ours, checked only by host builds and tests: close waits for every queue first, a failed start included; an open no longer keeps an earlier open's opt-in `XPU_*` settings; and the 27B runner waits for its queue before any host buffer a queued copy reads or writes goes away, on every error path, ending the process if the queue will not drain. Thanks to @Mirrdhyn.
- Qwen3.5-2B and Qwen3.8-27B serve on CUDA through the native server: each decode shape replays as a CUDA graph, the 27B drafts with DFlash2 in chains and trees, and concurrent requests share every round, each stream's reply equal to its run alone and to `"draft": false` on a GB10 (#836). Its author also built the native server and the CUDA suites on Windows through a platform layer, with the CUDA kernels on sm_89; we have no Windows check of our own (#836). Thanks to @BobClawblaw.
- The Qwen CUDA family reads mixed-width MLX checkpoints, 2 to 8 bits a module in groups of 64 (#849), and keeps 2, 3, 5 and 6-bit modules on tiles of their own width with their own prompt GEMM, rounding weights to bf16 once as the 4-bit prompt GEMM does (#859). Thanks to @BobClawblaw.
- Images for the Qwen CUDA family with `--vision`: the checkpoint's vision tower runs in TensorFold's kernels, its rows replace the image placeholders in the prompt chunks, and the prompt rotates at M-RoPE's t, h and w positions, with pictures decoded and prepared by the released `core/picture` and `core/qwen_vl` paths the Metal family uses (#885). Thanks to @BobClawblaw.
- The Qwen CUDA family keeps prompt states between turns in pinned host memory (8 GiB unless `--prompt-cache-gib` says otherwise, at most half of what the host has available), on the 128-row prompt grid; a state restores only when its geometry, cache format and every image's identity match the request's (#888). Ours: if a failed restore cannot drain its copies, dropping the state keeps its pinned memory, never freed, rather than freeing what the device may still read. Thanks to @BobClawblaw.
- `--kv-cache fp8` stores the Qwen CUDA family's attention caches as e4m3 rows with an fp32 scale each, about half the bytes of bf16; bf16 stays the default, and fp8 is off unless asked for (#886). Thanks to @BobClawblaw.
- Shared CUDA operators for every family: verified tree rounds (#734), tree attention and DFlash block attention (#746), SwiGLU and output gates, Gated DeltaNet inputs, QK norm and RoPE (#797), and sliding-window attention, sigmoid top-k MoE routing and combine, and dense rows (#798). `tf-cuda-test` checks the tree rounds byte for byte against the host's tree rounds and the arithmetic operators against float64 references. No family calls them yet. Thanks to @jschmied.
- A one-shot RoCE all-gather for two ranks, set up over the tensor-parallel group's NCCL communicator, with a two-rank test against NCCL's all-gather; no family calls it yet. Its design follows b12x's RoCE gather (#852). Thanks to @BobClawblaw.
- The lane core can take several queued prompts in one pass for a backend that batches them (`prefill_many`, `Engine.addStreams`); a failed pass leaves none of them prefilled. No served family batches prompts yet, so admission is unchanged (#853). Thanks to @BobClawblaw.
- Qwen3.8 Flash Next serves on CUDA through the native server from its MLX affine 4-bit, group-32 checkpoint with the MTP head, on kernels built in this repository: drafted windows, shared rounds of streams without drafts, kept prompt states and prompts a chunk at a time between other streams' rounds, all through the lane core, and past 2,048 keys attention reads the 512 blocks the model's indexer picks, up to the 262,144-token window. Each kernel keeps a row's sums in a fixed order; its authors measured on a GB10 that drafted replies equal `"draft": false`, concurrent replies their runs alone and resumed prompts fresh ones (`docs/recipes/flash-next-cuda.md`). A ModelOpt NVFP4 checkpoint on two local ranks is experimental (#952). Ours: every admitted stream that asks for drafts keeps them, where the fourth and later lost them; tensor-rank drafting with a prompt cache is refused at admission before the checkpoint or CUDA; #869's staging regression also fails the first copy and a drain before it succeeds (#869); and the kernel contract records #952's launches as nvcc 13.0, the release compiler, builds them, with nvcc 13.2's differences in its own set. Our kernel tests pass on an RTX PRO 6000 (sm_120) and a GB10 (sm_121), and on a GB10 we served the MLX 4-bit checkpoint with its MTP head: drafted replies equal `"draft": false` on all 17 cells, two and four concurrent replies equal their runs alone, and resumed chats equal cache-off ones on all 12 steps. Thanks to @ZackSample, @akol1, @AdrianBinDC, @Bizuayeu and @cwschroeder.
- Flash Next on CUDA takes `--vision`, experimental: images go through the Qwen-VL tower the Qwen CUDA family runs, now one shared module (`zig/src/vision/qwen_cuda.zig`) on rank zero, their rows replace the prompt's image placeholders over the four streams, M-RoPE positions reach attention, the block indexer and the MTP head, and a second rank receives the rows over NCCL. The checkpoint's preprocessor and processor files set the image processor through `core/qwen_vl`. A failed upload, plan, launch, wait or rank transfer now drains the stream, then the context, or ends the process, before the tower, an image plan or a lane frees memory the GPU may still use, for Qwen's tower too. On a GB10 the MLX 4-bit checkpoint's PNG image cells pass: an image reply drafted equals plain, four image requests together equal their runs alone, and a chat resumes past an image; JPEG needs libturbojpeg, which that machine lacked. The work is ours, built on #885's tower and #952. Thanks to @BobClawblaw and @akol1.
- The native CUDA build has EXL3 operators for a family's engine to call. `exl3.Dense` is a linear for every codebook and width, `mul1`'s half widths included, whose row keeps its bits at any row count, and prompts run in 128-row blocks of the same kernel. `qlinear` takes `.exl3` weights, with fp32 outputs as NVFP4 has them. `experts.Grouped` runs routed experts with each expert's gate, up and down at its own width. A checkpoint's trellis groups load from its safetensors headers. `tf-cuda-test exl3` checks the kernels against float64 references, and `exl3-tp2` checks the GLM split rules over two ranks. GLM-5.3-Flash's two-rank engine (below) runs its routed experts on this common module; no other family serves EXL3 natively yet (#837, #806, #741, #740). Ours: packed sign planes load, the scan, scratch and uploads keep their owners on every failure, and an upload needs a finite buffer budget. #806's kernels are a second linear behind `Dense`, used only when a caller asks, until the two are compared on the same checkpoint tensors. Grouped's up output is bf16, so it refuses a call that asks for the fp32 activation pipeline. Our kernel tests pass on an RTX PRO 6000 (sm_120) and a GB10 (sm_121). #837's kernels port the Python engine's EXL3 module (#42). Thanks to @justincasey, @BHCC2025 and @vcruz305.
- On Metal, the EXL3 projection decodes each weight tile once for a prompt window of 32 or more plain rows and shares it across four SIMD groups. Shorter windows, routed rows and indexed experts keep the eight-row kernel, and so does a device that cannot build the 128-thread pipeline. A row's bits stay the same either way (#729). Ours: the Metal projection loads from the same checkpoint scan and parts as CUDA, with its sign planes kept packed. Thanks to @mrv777.
- CUDA keeps a host-to-device copy's source until its stream proves the copy complete. Temporary uploads take pinned stages that one owner holds and hands to the next upload once their copies are done, so a decode step does not allocate and free pinned memory per copy; a wait that fails leaves the source quarantined instead of freeing it under a running copy, and `DeviceBuffer.fromHost` frees a buffer only after its copy completes. Uploads refuse a temporary source while a graph capture applies: replayed inputs live in stable stages.
- The EXL3 routed experts' SwiGLU clamps keep a NaN, as torch.clamp does, where they turned it into the limit; on the decode path a limit of 0 now means none, as it already did for prompts.
- GLM-5.3-Flash on two CUDA ranks reads Mia-AiLab's EXL3 checkpoint, its 4-bit mcg routed experts on the common EXL3 module, and with `--vision` takes images and video: rank 0's tower encodes them (video needs libtfvideo) and sends their rows and spans to the other rank. The ranks watch each other, so a rank that fails stops the other instead of leaving it waiting, and they agree on the checkpoint's bytes and every media limit before either loads; verify windows reach the decode buffers' 8 rows, and the bf16 linears run on glm_mm's tiles (#855, #976). Thanks to @BobClawblaw.
- Qwen3.6-35B-A3B serves on the Qwen CUDA engine through the native server: its routed experts in 4 or 3 bits behind a softmax router (the 3-bit planes run beside a 4-bit shared expert), the MTP layer beside the checkpoint drafting chains and trees, and the dense family's prompt cache and image tower. On a GB10 drafted replies equal plain ones, 17 of 17, and concurrent replies their runs alone (#856, #972). Ours: its router, top-k and combine launches and its MTP layer's are in the CUDA kernel contract, each against float64, with records for nvcc 13.0 and 13.2. Thanks to @BobClawblaw and @CanadaApollo6.
- A backend can ask the lane core to send a stream's first token before it prepares the first drafts, on a whole prompt pass or its last chunk; the Qwen CUDA backend does. The receipts are in `docs/receipts/qwen-cuda-first-token/` (#932). Thanks to @jrb-ai.
- Nemotron 3.5 Bolt (74B-A9B) serves on CUDA from NVIDIA's 24 GB checkpoint: puzzle layers, LatentMoE with its experts in GGML's IQ2_XXS and Q8_0 blocks on kernels of ours, image input, prompt states in pinned host memory on a card that cannot hold the prompt cache (the startup line says where they went), shared rounds of streams, and structured output and forced tool calls (#897, #938). On an RTX PRO 6000 (sm_120, nvcc 13.2) its drafted and plain replies equal those of #897's own build, concurrent replies equal their runs alone, and decode runs at 153 to 155 tok/s against that build's 144 to 146, with the same tokens. Ours: a failed load keeps its destinations and GPU parents until recovery proves them unused, the released router top-k kernel stays as it was beside a wide one for Bolt's 496 experts, and a negative `moe_latent_size` is refused before it becomes a width. Thanks to @BobClawblaw and @MiaAI-Lab.
- Nemotron Omni takes video through the RADIO tower: frames sampled into groups the tower reads as images, each with its time, and video tokens budgeted apart from image tokens; the reserved video token in user text stays text (#940). Ours: the video config, frame rate, indices, dimensions and grid are refused before any decode or cast. Thanks to @MiaAI-Lab and @cwschroeder.
- Gemma 4 serves natively on Metal: the 31B with its own dense and fused kernels on the tensor units, prompts in 512-row chunks, DFlash drafts, images and structured output (#879), and the 12B (`gemma4_unified`) with its unified text and image embedder and its MTP assistant as a native drafter (#975). Gemma's arithmetic runs in its own kernel copies, so no other family's bits move, and its kernels are in the Metal kernel contract (on M5, 12 of 12 at two seeds). Ours: `config.json`'s context and EOS ids are bounded before their casts, and the packed buffers and private pipelines keep one owner through every failure. Thanks to @BobClawblaw.
- Step-5 serves on one 256 GB Mac with Metal: a native `step5_vl` family with MTP drafts, images and kept prompt states (#903). Ours: the experts, every tensor's axes, the image tower's 667 tensors and the projector's width are checked before any model or device work. Thanks to @BobClawblaw.
- MiniMax H3 / FastH3 runs on Metal in bf16 as a test tool (`tf-h3-dit`): clips from text or from an image, any step count with its schedule and AdaLN tables, the released MiniMax-H3 weights, re-voicing a finished clip and Ref2VA's reference pictures, videos and audio (#961, #949, #950, #959, #960). Ours: the counts, held media, tiles, checkpoint shapes and schedule are checked before any shader or scratch, and a reschedule replaces every table together. Its tensor-unit kernels pass the Metal kernel contract on M5 (15 of 15). Thanks to @drowzeys.
- MiniMax H3 / FastH3's transformer blocks run on one CUDA device in bf16 as a C library for a host pipeline (`zig build tf-h3`, `libtf_h3.so`): products on the runtime's cuBLASLt with fp32 sums, the routed attention and every kernel under the CUDA kernel contract (#958, #947). Its sequence-parallel exchange over RoCE waits for a follow-up. Ours: checkpoint and projection sizes are checked before any allocation, only complete blocks load, and a failed load keeps its buffers and parents until their copies are proven done. Thanks to @drowzeys.
- Flash Next on Metal serves on shared sessions with an explicit `--parallel N` above one, their contexts following the memory the load leaves (`FLASHNEXT_SLOT_CONTEXTS` sizes them by hand): a lone request runs on the engine's own rounds and joins the shared ones when a second arrives, and every stream's MTP drafts go in one command buffer (chained batch drafts are experimental) (#606). Ours: `--parallel auto`, the default, keeps the single host, as on CUDA, since on an M3 Ultra a lone request decoded 7% slower in a shared session (plain 79.6 against 86.2 tok/s on the MLX 4-bit checkpoint) and the single host decodes as 1.0.12 does; with `--prompt-cache-gib` unset the shared host reserves the prompt cache first, by the single host's rule, and sizes the sessions from what is left; the shared rounds read the checkpoint's embedding, PLE and expert widths and the PLE scale as the single host does, so on the MLX 4-bit checkpoint plain, drafted and concurrent replies equal 1.0.12's. On a 256 GB M3 Ultra that checkpoint holds 129.6 GiB and 16 sessions of 65,568 tokens 31.6 GiB more. Thanks to @cwschroeder, @sethforprivacy and @BobClawblaw.
- Nemotron's FP8 and NVFP4 ModelOpt checkpoints serve on cards without thread-block clusters (sm_80 to sm_89): the lane matmul writes its split-K sums as fp32 partials and reduces them, each prompt segment through its own buffer's (#901). Ours: a reducing launch with no partials buffer is refused before it starts (`MissingLanePart`). On a GB10 Lightning's replies are unchanged and its device memory grows by the partials' 21.9 MB. Thanks to @BobClawblaw.
- A checkpoint file's content digest is kept on disk with its identity (device, inode, size and both times to the nanosecond, in `~/.cache/tensorfold/file-digests/v1`) and read again only when the file changes; a corrupt or foreign index is ignored. Every family's checkpoint identity reads its digests from there, and GLM-5.3-Flash's two CUDA ranks compare the checkpoint's whole content with them before either loads: on a GB10 pair the first start reads the 164 GB EXL3 checkpoint in 199 s, and a second start reads no shard (0.1 s) and is ready in 169 s against 370 s (#900). Thanks to @Bizuayeu.
- Fish Audio Drama 3 text to speech on CUDA and Metal: `POST /v1/audio/speech`, with `stream: true` and `response_format: pcm` sending raw PCM chunks as they decode, `drama3-generate` on both, and the checkpoint and `codec.pth` read without Python (#927, #956, #939, with #915 to #926 beneath them). Its authors measured on a GB10 a warm real-time factor of 0.655 against 1.087 before the one-row kernels, and a load of about 6 s against 53 s with direct I/O into staging pieces; a reply that reaches `max_frames` returns its clipped audio. The CUDA build keeps a process-wide cap on device and pinned host bytes (`setAllocationLimit`, unset by default). Ours: the stager's copies go through the copy-source owner, and Drama's kernels are in both kernel contracts with nvcc 13.2's records. Thanks to @plotarmordev and @drowzeys.
- Qwen-Image-2.1's transformer runs on Metal as a test tool (`tf-qwen-image`) on cases `tools/zig/qwen_image_case.py` makes with mflux, which also encodes the prompt and decodes the image (#948). Ours: the latent grid, scratch sizes and `eps` are checked before any device or checkpoint work. Thanks to @drowzeys.
- `tensorfold benchmark` reads `config.json` through the same 64 MiB metadata reader as `info` and `serve`. A missing config still reports an incomplete checkpoint; an oversized, unreadable or malformed one reports its own error with its size, where the file system gives one, and the limit. It read at most 4 MiB and called every failure an incomplete checkpoint (#934). Thanks to @jkuepker.
- When a benchmark run exits 2 it says why: incomplete attempts stay in the saved receipt, a cell has aggregates only when every repetition succeeds, and an early EOS is left out of full-length comparisons (#969). Thanks to @BlivionIaG.
- Nemotron on CUDA keeps the attention caches of the streams beside the engine's own in pages of one pool the core owns: a stream leases pages as its sequence grows and gives them back when it ends, a failed lease rolls back whole, and startup sizes the pool from what the model, the engine's own window, the prompt cache and one staging buffer leave, refusing with the bytes it needs when the room is short. `TF_KV_PAGES=0` gives every stream its own window as before, `TF_KV_PAGES=all` puts every stream on pages, and `TF_KV_POISON=1` fills freed pages so a stale read shows (#935). On GB10, Nemotron's replies, concurrent replies and prompt-cache counts are the same as before the pool in every mode, and Super VL's prefill is within 0.2% of 1.0.12's. Thanks to @jschmied and @cwschroeder.
- GLM keeps a long session's next turn on its cached prefix: when the pooled allocator needs room it moves the turn's own prefix instead of evicting it, and a restore that fails says once why, with the state's length and never its text (#966). Thanks to @tpischke and @heitke for the reports.
- GLM on Metal gathers a prompt's routed experts at any MLX affine width from 2 to 8 bits and group of 32 or 64, gate, up and down each their own; only uniform 4-bit and 2-bit experts in groups of 64 took the gathered path, so a checkpoint such as 3-bit gate and up with a 4-bit down fell back to the sorted kernels. All 468 checked layouts equal independent row products bit for bit (#965, #968). Thanks to @harrisonfriia.
- GLM on Metal runs a long prompt in chunks between the other streams' decode rounds, at most 256 rows a chunk while another stream decodes, so a stream beside a 100k-token prefill no longer stops until it ends; the shared fill selector lets the longest-waiting prompt in after eight skipped choices (#964). Thanks to @tpischke for the report.
- `tool_choice: "required"` and a named function constrain the reply on every backend with a token grammar, Gemma's and unrecognized chat templates included, and on Qwen3.8-27B on Metal, whose drafts grow under the same grammar so a drafted reply equals the plain one. A backend without a grammar refuses before generation and names itself (#918). Thanks to @astro-15-alive for the report.
- Qwen3.8-27B on Metal shares each round among up to 128 streams, each drafting its own DFlash2 tree in one drafter frame, verifies trees of up to 64 rows and takes prompts in 512-row passes; a lone stream also copies its context's continuation into its drafts. Drafted replies equal plain ones, and each concurrent stream equals its reply alone. On an M5 Ultra with TensorFold's MLX 4-bit checkpoint and z-lab's DFlash2 drafter at `--parallel 8`, against 1.0.12 on the same machine and on the cells #918 asked for: cold prefill 931 to 1,349 tok/s at 8,192 tokens and 891 to 1,250 at 30,720; 512-token greedy decode 116 to 126 tok/s for prose and 386 to 432 for code (97 to 104 and 305 to 325 at t=0.7); eight streams at once 222-239 to 314 tok/s together greedy and 232-235 to 292-296 at t=0.7. A warm resume's first token stays at 0.12 to 0.18 s. The second stream's DeltaNet states are allocated at start, so at `--parallel 2` the server holds 26.5 GB where it held 22.6 GB. Its five register-slot DeltaNet pipelines are in the Metal kernel contract against float64, on an M3 Ultra and an M5 Ultra, with their M1 and M2 thread limits from offline compiles.
- RUNBOOK.md's heat bands section adds one measured run: on three GB10s serving GLM-5.3-Flash NVFP4 at TP=3, a 1,035,295-token prompt without the bands kept one host's hottest zone at 94 C or more for most of the prompt, and `TF_HEAT_HIGH=92` with `TF_HEAT_LOW=88` held all three at 93.0 C or under, the first token 10% later (#988). Thanks to @Bizuayeu.
- `docs/receipts/gfx1151-qwen-833bbfcb/` qualifies 1.0.12's opt-in HIP build on one gfx1151 (Radeon 8060S) for Qwen3.5-9B, Qwen3.6-35B-A3B with MTP and Qwen3.8-27B: 130 model checks pass and 11 are skipped, every HTTP reply's full token digest is kept, and the 27B's cancellation failure and the 9B's and 27B's drafting comparisons, which did not run, stay listed as open (#994). Thanks to @ThinkOffApp.
- Nemotron's latent MoE on CUDA: every segment of a prompt split into segments reads the shared-expert plans and pair ids its owner builds, where the other segments' buffers carved fresh ones that nothing wrote (#996). Thanks to @plotarmordev.
- The benchmark's refusal-body test holds its fake server's sender on an event after the header, so it proves the body is not buffered instead of waiting 20 ms (#998). Thanks to @cwschroeder.
- Nemotron 3.5 Super on CUDA has decode-sized forms of its latent top-k (each expert ranked on a thread of its own) and of the checkpoint's NVFP4 experts (32 columns a warp, four steps in flight), each bit-equal to the kernel it replaces and in the kernel contract, with 1,024 experts, 32 picks and rows without a finite score among its cases (#999). Thanks to @plotarmordev.
- Nemotron's tensor-parallel worker sends a job's first message with one row, so a decode step is one message where it carried 16 rows of padding; larger jobs still send their second message (#1000). Thanks to @easthambuilds.
- WebP images decode through libwebp 1.6.0's `libwebpdecoder`, loaded at the first WebP from `TENSORFOLD_LIBWEBP`, `lib/` or the system's library path and refused by name when missing or another release, to the RGB bytes Pillow 13.0.0 gives the same file (lossy and lossless, with and without alpha, EXIF-oriented); animated WebP is refused, and TensorFold ships no copy of the library (#1004). Thanks to @BobClawblaw.
- GLM-5.3-Flash on CUDA keeps its index keys and gates in 4,096-row rings that rebase before a pass would cross them, 0.674 GiB a stream at a 40,960-token window, so `--parallel 16` fits and 16 streams share one verify of 2 rows each (#984). Ours: an MTP rebase keeps the whole verify window before its cursor, so a rejected proposal rolls back into rows the rings still hold. Thanks to @BobClawblaw.
- GLM-5.3-Flash on CUDA can pack its dense linears to 4, 6 or 8 bits at load (`TF_GLM_DENSE_BITS`, `TF_GLM_HEAD_BITS`; 16, bf16, stays the default), the ranks agreeing on the bits; `TF_ROCE_MAX_KB` caps a RoCE gather's shard, 4,096 KiB by default; and NCCL's cuMem allocations are off on the ranks, which on GB10 exhausted the driver's pool under load (#995). Ours: a malformed, zero or overflowing cap is refused before any allocation, and the packed launches are in the kernel contract with their records measured on nvcc 13.0 and 13.2. Thanks to @BobClawblaw.
- GLM-5.3-Flash on CUDA drafts with DFlash2 (`--drafter`): taps after five layers, per-slot context rings, a block pass split across the ranks and drafts sized by their chances against measured window costs, its linears at 4 bits by default (`--drafter-bits 4|0`) (#979). Ours: the rank buffers share one layout for 1 to 16 ranks, a failed load frees what it allocated, and the drafter's launches are in the kernel contract with their records measured on nvcc 13.0 and 13.2. Thanks to @BobClawblaw.
- Gemma 4 on Metal reads every MLX affine width the core supports, each module's width from the config, with stacks of mixed widths in runs; the 12B converter takes mlx-lm's mixed recipe, and the recipes record the widths checked against the oracles, mlx-lm and transformers (#977). Ours: a failure at any width frees the sets built before it, and every dense and embedding width is in the kernel contract, pending its records. Thanks to @BobClawblaw and @gilby.
- GLM's prompt cache spares a kept state that an outstanding lookup still restores, and a failed restore says why (#1007). Ours: eviction relocates the selected prefix before it takes a borrowed neighbour, and a failure is reported once. Thanks to @heitke.
- The app leaves the engine's prompt cache to the native planner instead of asking for 8 GiB, which the engine refused on a Mac whose load and streams left less room (reported in #989). Thanks to @Deesha08.
- `--mtp-drafts` and `--mtp-confidence` set a CUDA Nemotron stream's MTP depth and the cumulative confidence its drafts need (#997). Ours: they reach the shared lane rounds as well as a lone stream. Thanks to @plotarmordev.
- Nemotron 3.5 Super on CUDA verifies windows of up to 32 rows, 31 MTP drafts a round, with its Mamba scan in stages of 32 held to its host twin's bytes in the kernel contract, and a lone stream's copied and Recall chains start at 15 tokens (#1006). Ours: a checkpoint without an MTP head keeps its shared copy lane, and only Super's latent models widen to 32 rows. Thanks to @plotarmordev.
- Nemotron's latent shared-expert plans for a decode window are built once at buffer load, one per row count, and on one GPU the shared expert runs on the side stream beside the routed experts (#1002). Ours: shared windows of several sequences and two-rank windows keep the earlier schedule. Thanks to @plotarmordev.
- An attached benchmark run reads the server's `GET /v1/benchmark/manifest`, so a two-rank CUDA run can be ranked when the manifest's facts pass the receipt checks (#980). Ours: an unknown backend is never attested as CUDA, and an attached run whose accepted manifest lacks its rank count or revision says it is unranked. Thanks to @easthambuilds.
- Nemotron 3.5 Super on CUDA drafts with its MTP head over the embedded draft vocabulary's FP8 output rows: a stream drafts on the subset head while its last 16 context tokens are draft ids, else on the whole head (#1011). Ours: a failed head construction drains before it frees anything, and gathered rows are checked before any read. Thanks to @plotarmordev.
- Nemotron 3.5 Super's checkpoint prompt passes on CUDA run the FP8 layers in 128-row tiles and the NVFP4 experts in 128-pair by 128-column tiles, up writing down's NVFP4 rows and one GPU's down summing the width shares into bf16, and router logits past 32 rows run on 128-row by 128-expert tiles, each bit-equal to the kernels it replaces in the kernel contract (#1008). Thanks to @plotarmordev.
- Windows ARM64 builds the native CUDA engine from source, with NVIDIA's RTX Spark N1X (sm_121) among its targets; the device results in `docs/WINDOWS.md` are the contributor's reports (#1010). Ours: copy custody takes its locks and waits from std.Io's threaded mutex and condition, which Windows has; a Windows integrated GPU sizes its pool from CUDA's counts; and a library path resolves in full before it loads. Thanks to @mweinbach and @BobClawblaw.
- The lane core's allocate cuts a grafted window's held count and branch start with its rows, so a shared round that trims a window with suffix-match grafts replies as the one-token decode does (#1013). Ours: the family trim path takes the same cut, so a one-draft cut keeps one draft and the first remaining graft keeps its index. Thanks to @chaog992.
- In the lane core, the grammar's own drafts after a drafted window that is a chain but carries tree parents, as a pruned tree or a suffix-match graft from the pending token can, extend that chain. The core read their parents past the end of the window's list and failed the round (`ParentAfterChild`), which the 27B's required and named tool calls on Metal met with drafts on.

### Moving to 1.1.1

These changes are not shipped in 1.1.0 and remain conditional on their final gates; a failed gate moves the work to the following patch release.

- Living Weights implementation and safety work, including retention, rounded commit/rollback, conversation and supplied-question work, Flash Next/Qwen CUDA learners, lesson progress and save/restart pieces, is in 1.1.1 (#876, #877, #878, #891, #905, #992, #895, #896, #911). Model, retention and save/restart qualification remains pending. The #1029 lesson-counter repair is excluded and remains held: rejected lessons must not shift the prompts used for later training and checks. The existing learner's #910 correction above remains in 1.1.0. Thanks to @ZackSample, @philip-pentatonic, @akol1 and @BobClawblaw.
- Flash Next on Metal's images/video and million-token window (#986, #1005), still held for the rotary-bound contract and actual probe rerun. Its separate shared-session cache-count and lone-decode repair is held for a later 1.1.x, outside the current 1.1.1 series. Thanks to @BobClawblaw, @cwschroeder and @sethforprivacy.
- The larger Qwen CUDA lane rounds, verification trees, batched drafting and lone-stream speed work (#978), distinct from the Metal 27B speed work shipped above. Thanks to @BobClawblaw and the shared CUDA contributors.
- DeepSeek-V4.1 runtime and family integration (#635, #636, #637, #663, #682, #667, #668, #671, #674, #686, #688, #691, #693, #694, #695, #699, #702, #703, #707, #727, #824, #825, #826, #827), and Kolibri text CUDA (#991). Shared contracts do not announce a complete DeepSeek Metal backend. Thanks to @jayleaton, @akol1, @ajensenwaud and @jschmied, retaining the common operator attributions.
- GLM CUDA fresh-prompt batching, prompt scoring, structured output and required/named tool-call follow-ups (#1014, #1016, #1017, #1018). Thanks to @BobClawblaw and @Bizuayeu.

MiMo-V2.6-Flash (#728) is held for a later 1.1.x, outside the current 1.1.1 series: its eight native CUDA launches, then its model and device gates, remain owed, and its source in this release is not a shipped native backend. Thanks to @ajensenwaud and @jayleaton.

Known issues:
- GLM-5.3-Flash's ModelOpt NVFP4 checkpoint on two CUDA ranks was not run for this release: on two GB10s the weights leave no room for its streams beside the memory reserve. Code that path shares changed in this release (NVFP4 scale planes and their tensor-parallel slices, the copy-source owner, NCCL's allocations and the RoCE cap on the ranks), so it stays a known issue until its regression run; the EXL3 checkpoint is the two-rank path measured for this release.
- The reported M3 Ultra peak for Qwen3.8-27B on Metal is 4.1 GB higher than 1.0.12 because the second DeltaNet bank is allocated at startup; reducing that allocation remains follow-up work.
- Flash Next on Metal with explicit --parallel 2 or more retains a cache-count/performance issue: on an M3 Ultra with the MLX 4-bit checkpoint, 9 of 12 chat steps reused 17 to 312 fewer tokens than the single host, with the same output tokens, and a lone request decoded 7% slower. The default --parallel auto uses the single host and matched 1.0.12's cache counts; the separate shared-session repair is held for a later 1.1.x and is absent from the current 1.1.1 series.

## 1.0.12

- Nemotron serves MLX checkpoints with a width per module on Metal: TensorFold's oQ4, oQ6 and oQ8 quants, and any MLX affine checkpoint at 2 to 8 bits (not 7) in groups of 32, 64 or 128. 1.0.11 refused every one of them at `config.json` ("the native Nemotron kernels read every matrix at the checkpoint's 4-bit in groups of 64"), and refused a uniform 8-bit checkpoint with a reason about its shapes. A matrix at 4-bit in groups of 64 runs on the kernels it ran on before. In decode, every other width runs on the shared row kernels. Prompt chunks past 128 rows run the other dense matrices on simd_qmm_bits' tiles where their groups are 64, and the routed experts on Flash Next's width-general expert gathers, compiled at load for each width the checkpoint uses. The embedding has a lookup for any width, a mixed q/k/v stack runs member by member, and the routed experts run by expert id. On an M3 Ultra, oQ4, oQ6 and oQ8 each gave drafted replies equal to `"draft": false` on 17 prompts, 2 and 4 concurrent streams equal to their solo runs, and 12 resumed chat turns equal to a cache-off server's. Teacher-forced against mlx_lm's forward of the same checkpoint on 3,072 positions, their mean KL is 0.0050 to 0.0054 nats a token, with the same top token at 96.2% to 97.0% of positions; the 4-bit checkpoint gives 0.0056 and 96.2% through the same tool. Prompts of 8,192 to 32,768 tokens read at 0.96x to 0.99x mlx_lm's rate on the same checkpoint. Greedy prose decodes at 162 tok/s with oQ4, 117 with oQ6 and 111 with oQ8, against 188 with the 4-bit checkpoint, and greedy code at 249, 183 and 174 against 268. The oQ checkpoints carry no MTP head, so copy `mtp-4bit.safetensors` from the 4-bit checkpoint into the folder to draft.
- For Nemotron's MLX affine checkpoints, CUDA and the Intel GPU engine read 4-bit in groups of 64 only, and refuse a checkpoint at any other width by naming its first such module; NVIDIA's ModelOpt checkpoints are read as before. The Intel GPU engine had no check before and would have read such a checkpoint at the wrong width. On Metal, `--slide` needs every matrix at 4-bit in groups of 64, and loading saved learned neurons needs the shared expert's up and down matrices at that width; both refuse by naming the module.
- `tensorfold-native capabilities --json` lists every MLX affine width for Nemotron, Flash Next and qwen3_5 checkpoints on Metal. The app's download check compares a checkpoint's top-level width with that list, which named only 4-bit group 64 for qwen3_5 and 6-bit group 32 for Flash Next, so it turned away Jundot's Qwen3.8-27B oQ6e and every Flash Next oQ checkpoint that the engine serves. On an M3 Ultra, Jundot's 27B oQ4e and oQ6e with DFlash2 and a Flash Next oQ5e each gave drafted replies equal to `"draft": false`, concurrent streams equal to their solo runs, and resumed chats equal to a cache-off server's.
- Nemotron 3.5 Super VL reads images on CUDA with `--vision`, through the checkpoint's own RADIO tower and pixel-shuffle projector in TensorFold's kernels (`zig/src/families/radio/`). The processor is the checkpoint's: each image becomes one tile of up to 13,312 patches of 16 pixels (an even number each way; at least 1,024, except that a picture smaller than that sent with `"detail": "low"` keeps its own grid, down to 2 x 2 patches and one token), resized with torch's antialiased bicubic on float samples and normalized with CLIP's mean and deviation, and on a GB10 its pixels equal the checkpoint processor's bit for bit on six pictures, five resizes and 3,000 patch grids. An image takes a quarter of its patches as tokens (at most 3,328), written between `<img>` and `</img>`. The tower runs the checkpoint's bf16 weights as stored: against a float32 run of the checkpoint's own modules on the same pixels, its rows' error is 0.99x to 1.03x the modules' own bf16 run's. Served on a GB10, image requests drafted equal `"draft": false`, four concurrent image requests equal their solo runs, a later turn resumes past its image while another image under the same placeholders resumes only the text before it, and two GB10s give one GB10's replies word for word. A 13,000-patch image's tower and projector take 0.87 s on a GB10. Video and audio parts are refused for now. The vision towers' CUDA glue is shared (`cuda/vit.zig`): LFM2-VL's moved there unchanged, and a new attention kernel for 80-wide heads gives the old kernel's bytes 1.35x faster.
- Nemotron 3.5 Super VL serves on CUDA from NVIDIA's NVFP4 checkpoint (`nemotron_h_omni`), on one GB10 or two. Its latent MoE (512 routed experts, 22 a token, read and written in a 1,024-wide latent between two bf16 projections, beside a shared expert) runs in TensorFold's kernels with each expert's width cut into two shares that every setup sums in share order, so two GB10s give one GB10's tokens: all 17 replies to the eight release prompts (greedy, seeded sampled, thinking on) matched, in both precisions below. On two GB10s rank 0 runs the model and serves, and rank 1 holds only its share of the experts (31.5 GiB, against rank 0's 40.1) and answers each MoE layer's job over NCCL. Before any weight loads the ranks agree on the build, the model, its cut, the precision and the checkpoint's layout (config.json, the index, each shard's name, size and header); while the weights load each rank hashes every byte of every shard it holds (sha256, each file checked against its Hugging Face blob name in a snapshot), and the ranks compare those digests before rank 0 serves, so a copy one bit off anywhere stops both ranks before serving, with its name (a GB10 hashes the 70 GiB checkpoint in about 22 s, at the same time as it loads its weights); a TCP line opened before NCCL then carries ready and error records, so a rank that fails stops the other with its error's name, and one that dies stops the other within about a second instead of leaving it waiting. Served at a 16,384-token window, drafted replies equal `"draft": false`, 2 and 4 concurrent streams equal their solo runs, and chats with regenerate, edit and delete equal a cache-off server's replies, on one GB10 and on two. MTP drafts give 38.2 tok/s on one GB10 and 46.8 on two (greedy, mean of eight prompts), against 18.8 and 20.2 with `"draft": false`; the MTP layer drafts with the target's own output head. Super VL's `--vision-image-tokens` defaults to 13,312, so up to four images keep the size NVIDIA's processor gives them: four full-size pictures on one GB10 took 13,124 prompt tokens and 11.45 s to the first token. The [recipe](docs/recipes/nemotron-3.5-super-vl.md) has both setups.
- On CUDA, `--precision checkpoint|full` chooses an NVIDIA NVFP4 checkpoint's math. `checkpoint`, Super VL's default on Blackwell, is NVIDIA's own: e4m3 rows by each FP8 layer's stored input scale into an FP8 x FP8 mma, and NVFP4 rows (an e4m3 scale every 16 values, e2m1 rounded to nearest) into its NVFP4 experts on the block-scaled FP4 mma (`cuda/ck.zig`, compute capability 12.x). `full` keeps bf16 activations (W4A16 and W8A16).
